# The reported accuracies against the reconstruction, by variant (supplement)

Conditions: `experiments/conditions/variants.json` (SHA-256 `24516ef8480230701fd7525eb826f99ef2de28b120032582e5bb650a6bfb8de3`). Specification SHA-256 `0ab265408e73e8a7b75e58f60df990ebd718db0f2b8b3b3c27d501bcdad096dc`. Script SHA-256 `da716e3a63b06f825e7c5d56ac54fe2eedb34a99cb69939fb8e4d8835fe60134`. Commit c5e691c (dirty: False). numpy 2.5.4, scipy 1.18.1, scikit-learn 1.9.1, Python 3.13.12. Run started 2026-10-26T06:30:45+00:00 and finished 2026-10-26T08:17:31+00:00 (Darwin arm64).

All values come from the reconstructed generator, not from the original code. The list of variants was fixed after exploratory computations over overlapping grids had been seen (stated in the conditions). Nothing here is evidence for the claims of the main text.

## Reading of the conditions fixed before the run

- Dataset: for each (noise, jitter w, warp, N) and dataset replicate r (0-19), 60 surfaces per regime at the corners (1, -1) and (-1, 1), drawn as in atact.classify.make_dataset: rng = default_rng(r); for each surface V and A are the corner plus U(-w, w), clipped to [-1, 1]; the surface seed is 42 + index (0-119), the same in every replicate (assumption A5), so the replicates differ only in the jitter draws. The field is atact.field.procedural_field with RenderConfig(n=N, noise=noise, warp=warp), maxabs normalisation, four octaves, product form; with warp > 0 the two warp layers are drawn before the octave phases (A3).
- Descriptors: raw uses H; hann uses the windowed array of S3.1 (atact.spectral.windowed), and both descriptors are computed on that array: Sq = atact.descriptors.rq and the centroid = atact.descriptors.centroid_variant with one of the 32 definitions. 'center' and 'rescale' of a definition therefore act on the windowed array under hann (it is already centred, so center = False adds nothing there except through rescale). Sq does not depend on the centroid definition, so the Sq-only accuracy is computed once per (noise, w, window, warp, N).
- Cross-validation: RepeatedStratifiedKFold(n_splits=5, n_repeats=20, random_state=0) for every variant and replicate, a pipeline StandardScaler + SVC(kernel='linear', C=1) on the single descriptor; the accuracy of a replicate is the mean over the 100 test folds (as atact.classify.evaluate).
- Delta = accuracy - reported value, for sq_only (reported 0.916) and centroid_only (0.742). The point estimate is the mean of Delta over the 20 replicates; the 95% interval is the percentile interval of the 20 values (numpy.percentile, linear interpolation). The binomial width shown beside it is 1.96 sqrt(p (1 - p) / 120) at the reported p, for reference only.
- Reading rule (fixed in the conditions): a variant can explain a reported value if the interval of Delta for that column contains 0. Direction: whether the mean Sq-only accuracy exceeds the mean centroid-only accuracy.
- Warp check: every condition and jitter w of k5_classification.json at N = 128, processing raw, representation H, the four descriptor sets and both classifiers, with the 480 pairs and seeds of the K5 experiment (pair i: seed 1000 + i; iii_null group 1: 50000 + i) and the jitter streams of K5 (k5_classification.jitter_u). The field is procedural_field with warp 0.25. As an implementation control the same is computed with warp 0 by procedural_field and compared with the stored accuracies of K5 (results/k5_classification.json), which used procedural_field_separable; the two must agree exactly. The out-of-fold accuracy uses the folds and classifiers of K5; no bootstrap or permutation is run for the check.
- Warp and the draws: with warp > 0 the two warp layers consume the first draws of default_rng(seed), so the octave phases differ from those at warp 0 for the same seed. The difference between warp 0.25 and warp 0 therefore mixes the effect of the warp with a redraw of the surfaces. No interval is computed for it; the 95% pair-bootstrap interval stored by K5 for the warp-0 accuracy is shown beside it as a reference for the size of sampling variation.
- Implementation control: if any warp-0 accuracy differs from the stored K5 value, the run stops before writing the results (the variants are kept in results/partial/ for diagnosis). The warp check runs first, the variants second; the variants are written to results/partial/ as soon as they are finished.
- Counting: the Sq-only accuracy does not depend on the centroid definition, so each of the 72 combinations of (noise, w, warp, N, window) appears in 32 rows with the same Sq-only values. Counts for Sq only are given both as combinations (of 72) and as rows (of 2304).

## Conclusions (written after the run)

This section was written by hand after the run and after the generated results were committed unedited (e41568a). It does not change any criterion. The conditions state no numerical predictions; they fix the reading rule, the direction check and the warp check. The record of the run says "dirty: False" at c5e691c. The counts by jitter width and the order comparisons below were taken from the rows of this file after the run; they are not readouts named in the conditions.

**Implementation control (holds).** The accuracies at warp 0 equal the stored K5 accuracies in all 119 rows (0 mismatches), so procedural_field at warp 0 and procedural_field_separable agree on the K5 data.

**Reported values.** 101 of the 2304 variants contain 0 in both intervals and reproduce the reported direction; all 101 use warp 0.25 and jitter w = 1.25 or 1.5. The rule therefore admits variants that can explain both reported values, but it does not single one out: the 101 cover all three noise levels, both grids, both window treatments and 28 of the 32 centroid definitions.

**What decides the Sq-only value.** The Sq-only accuracy is set by the jitter width, not by the warp. Its mean is 0.997-0.998 at w = 1.0 and 0.988-0.992 with the warp, 0.944-0.952 at w = 1.25 (0.940-0.948 with the warp), and 0.873-0.878 at w = 1.5 (0.874-0.879 with the warp); the reported 0.916 lies between the last two. At w = 1.25 and 1.5 the end of the interval of Delta nearest 0 stays 0.002-0.009 short of it at warp 0 and passes it by 0.0001-0.015 at warp 0.25. That no warp-0 combination contains 0 is a margin of a few thousandths at the end of an interval from 20 replicates, not a difference between the warps in the mean accuracy.

**Warp check (does not hold).** The conditions describe this computation as the check that the warp does not change the K5 results. It does change them. At warp 0.25 the accuracies differ from those at warp 0 by up to 0.347 (condition i_V, centroid, threshold: 0.985 to 0.639 at w = 0.1). The centroid under i_V falls from 0.93-0.99 to 0.64-0.66 at every w, Sq under i_A from 0.88-0.96 to 0.70-0.74, and the centroid under ii_s_gamma rises from 0.84-0.96 to 0.98-1.00. The order of the descriptor sets also changes in some rows: under i_A the centroid set C is below the band set B at warp 0 and above it at warp 0.25 (for example at w = 0.3, svm: C - B = -0.047 at warp 0 and +0.019 at warp 0.25). Under ii_s and the corners the rows change by at most 0.043, and the controls stay near 0.5 (the same-seed control exactly 0.5). As fixed in the conditions, the difference mixes the warp with a redraw of the surface phases, and no interval of the difference was computed. For scale, 82 of the 119 warp-0.25 accuracies lie outside the 95% pair-bootstrap interval stored by K5 for the warp-0 accuracy; this interval describes the sampling variation of one accuracy, not of the difference.

**Consequence for the main text.** The levels of Table 1, and the order of the descriptor sets that the main text derives from K1-K4, hold for the field without warp, which is the field the paper fixes. With the warp that the preprint allows, the levels change by up to 0.35 and the order changes in some cells. Whether the original generator used a warp, and with what amplitude, is not known.
